P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Next, the assumption that
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}
has a density that is continuous and positive in a neighborhood of
\beta_{0}
guarantees that there exists a positive sequence
c_{n,2}
such that
c_{n,2}\rightarrow 0
and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)}{\inf_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)}-1\right|\leq c_{n,2}.

As a result, with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching one, the following quantities

\displaystyle(1+c_{n,1})(1+c_{n,2})\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}

and

\displaystyle[(1+c_{n,1})(1+c_{n,2})]^{-1}\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}

are upper and lower bounds, respectively, for
F_{n,2}(A)
, conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Since
c_{n,1},c_{n,2}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{A}\left|F_{n,2}(A)-\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 4.
Recalling that
\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}=\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})/\sqrt{n}
, I complete the square to obtain

\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\left(\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}\right)^{2}\right)\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}n\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\left(\beta-\beta_{0}-\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\right)\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}^{2}\right).

It then follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)d\beta}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}n\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\left(\beta-\beta_{0}-\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\right)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}n\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\left(\beta-\beta_{0}-\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}}\right)^{2}\right)d\beta}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta}

(12)

for all events
A
, where
\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}
is the density of
\mathcal{N}(\beta_{0}+\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}/\sqrt{n},(n\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0}))^{-1})
. The denominator satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta=\Phi\left(\frac{M_{n}-\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}}\right)-\Phi\left(\frac{-M_{n}-\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}}\right)

where
\Phi(\cdot)
is standard normal cumulative distribution function. Following Part 4 of Assumption
1
, I can apply Chebyshev’s inequality the Strong Law of Large Numbers to conclude that

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}\tilde{I}_{0}(m_{0})

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
\tilde{I}_{0}(m_{0})=\sigma_{01}^{-2}E_{P_{0}}[(X-m_{02}(W))^{2}]
. An application of the Continuous Mapping Theorem (valid by Part 2 of Assumption
1
) then leads to the conclusion that

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{n}^{-1}(m_{0})\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}\tilde{I}_{0}^{-1}(m_{0})

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, Chebyshev’s inequality, Assumption
1
, and the Strong Law of Large Numbers implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})=O_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and, as a result, the Continuous Mapping Theorem implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}=O_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since
M_{n}\rightarrow\infty
, it then follows that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta=1+o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1).

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{A}\left|\frac{\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta}{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta}-\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta\leq\int_{A}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta

for all events
A
, I need to show that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta\geq\int_{A}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta\ \forall\ A\right)\longrightarrow 1

(13)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Indeed, this implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{A}\left|\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta-\int_{A}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0,

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and, as a result, an application of the triangle inequality leads to the conclusion that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Pi(\beta\in A|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},W_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})-\mathcal{N}\left(\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}\right)\right|\right|_{TV}\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. For (
13
), the events
A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})
and
A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}
define a partition of
A
meaning that

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{A}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta-\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta

\displaystyle\quad\geq\int_{A}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta-\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta

for all events
A
, where the lower bound holds because

\displaystyle\int_{A\cap B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta\leq\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta

for all events
A
using the monotonicity of probability measures. An earlier argument showed that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta=1+o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1),

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, so the complement rule for probability measures implies that

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\phi_{\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}}(\beta)d\beta=o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. As such, I conclude that (
13
) holds and complete the proof.
∎

A.2
Proof of Corollaries
1
and
2

Proof of Corollary
1
.

Theorem
1
implies that for every
\zeta>0
,
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n}(\zeta))\rightarrow 0
for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where

\displaystyle A_{n}(\zeta)=\left\{\left|\left|\Pi(\beta\in A|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})-\mathcal{N}\left(\beta_{0}+\frac{\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}}{\sqrt{n}},\frac{1}{n}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})^{-1}\right)\right|\right|_{TV}\geq\zeta\right\}.

Since
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}\mapsto P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n}(\xi))
is bounded, the bounded convergence theorem implies that, for each
\zeta>0
,
E_{P_{0,W}^{\infty}}[P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n}(\zeta))]\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since i.i.d sampling and the law of iterated expectations implies
E_{P_{0,W}^{\infty}}[P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n}(\zeta))]=P^{n}_{0}(A_{n}(\zeta))
, I am able to conclude the claim of Corollary
1
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
2
.

Let
F_{\mathcal{N}(0,\tilde{I}_{n}^{-1}(m_{0}))}(\cdot)
be the cumulative distribution function of
\mathcal{N}(0,\tilde{I}_{n}^{-1}(m_{0}))
and let
\hat{\beta}_{n}=\beta_{0}+\tilde{\Delta}_{n,0}/\sqrt{n}
. Theorem
1
implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in\mathbb{R}}\left|\Pi(\sqrt{n}(\beta-\hat{\beta}_{n})\leq b|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})-F_{\mathcal{N}(0,\tilde{I}_{n}^{-1}(m_{0}))}(b)\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since the set of continuity points of the Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,\tilde{I}_{n}^{-1}(m_{0}))
is
\mathbb{R}
, I can apply Lemma 21.2 of
Van der Vaart 1998
and the equivariance of quantiles under monotonic transformations to conclude that

\displaystyle c_{n}(q)=\hat{\beta}_{n}+\Phi^{-1}\left(q\right)\sqrt{\frac{\tilde{I}_{n}^{-1}(m_{0})}{n}}+o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

holds for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

A.3
Proofs of Lemmas
1
and
2

Proof of Lemma
1
.

The proof has four steps with each step corresponding to the appropriate part of the lemma (i.e., Step 1 verifies Part 1 of the lemma and so on).

Step 1.
Let
\tilde{\ell}(Y,X,W,\beta,m)=(Y-m_{1}(W)-(X-m_{2}(W))\beta)(X-m_{2}(W))/\sigma_{01}^{2}
for each
(\beta,m)
. Since
Y_{i}=m_{01}(w_{i})+(X_{i}-m_{02}(w_{i}))\beta_{0}+U_{i}
and
X_{i}=m_{02}(w_{i})+\varepsilon_{i2}
, I know that

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i},\beta_{0},m)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{01}^{2}}\left(Y_{i}-m_{1}(w_{i})-(X_{i}-m_{2}(w_{i}))\beta_{0}\right)\left(X_{i}-m_{2}(w_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{01}^{2}}\left(m_{01}(w_{i})-m_{1}(w_{i})-(m_{02}(w_{i})-m_{2}(w_{i}))\beta_{0}+U_{i}\right)\left(m_{02}(w_{i})-m_{2}(w_{i})+\varepsilon_{i2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma_{01}^{2}}\left\{(m_{01}(w_{i})-m_{1}(w_{i}))(m_{02}(w_{i})-m_{2}(w_{i}))-(m_{02}(w_{i})-m_{2}(w_{i}))^{2}\beta_{0}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.+(m_{02}(w_{i})-m_{2}(w_{i}))(U_{i}-\beta_{0}\varepsilon_{i2})+(m_{01}(w_{i})-m_{1}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i2}+U_{i}\varepsilon_{i2}\right\}.

Observing that
\tilde{\ell}(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i},\beta_{0},m_{0})=U_{i}\varepsilon_{i2}/\sigma_{01}^{2}
, the previous derivation implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{01}^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sqrt{n}\langle m_{1}-m_{01},m_{2}-m_{02}\rangle_{n,2}-\sqrt{n}||m_{2}-m_{02}||_{n,2}^{2}\beta_{0}-G_{n}^{(1)}(m)-G_{n}^{(2)}(m),

where I recall that the empirical processes
\{G_{n}^{(1)}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
\{G_{n}^{(2)}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
are given by

\displaystyle G_{n}^{(1)}(m)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{1}(w_{i})-m_{01}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i2}

and

\displaystyle G_{n}^{(2)}(m)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\{\left(m_{2}(w_{i})-m_{02}(w_{i})\right)\left(U_{i}-\beta_{0}\varepsilon_{i2}\right)\right\}

for
m\in\mathcal{M}
. Applying Assumption
4
, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}|G_{n}^{(1)}(m)|=o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}|G_{n}^{(2)}(m)|=o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Moreover, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the definition of
B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})
reveals that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left|\sqrt{n}\langle m_{1}-m_{01},m_{2}-m_{02}\rangle_{n,2}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\sqrt{n}||m_{2}-m_{02}||_{n,2}^{2}\lesssim\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}.

Using Assumption
3
, I know that
\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}=o(1)
. Consequently, when combined with the arguments above, I am able to conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 2.
Since
X_{i}=m_{02}(w_{i})+\varepsilon_{i2}
, it follows that

\displaystyle(X_{i}-m_{2}(w_{i}))^{2}=\left(m_{2}(w_{i})-m_{02}(w_{i})\right)^{2}-2(m_{2}(w_{i})-m_{02}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i2}+\varepsilon_{i2}^{2},

and, as a result, I obtain

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})=\frac{1}{\sigma_{01}^{2}}||m_{2}-m_{02}||_{n,2}^{2}-\frac{2}{\sigma_{01}^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{2}(w_{i})-m_{02}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i2}.

Applying Assumption
4
, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D,\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{2}(w_{i})-m_{02}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i2}\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, using the definition of
B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})
and Assumption
3
, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}||m_{2}-m_{02}||_{n,2}^{2}\lesssim\delta_{n}^{2}=o(1)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently, I conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left|\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 3.
I add and subtract
\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})
and apply the law of total probability to conclude that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)<\underline{c}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)<\underline{c}_{0},\ \tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\geq\overline{c}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)<\underline{c}_{0},\ \tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})<\overline{c}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}(\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0}))<0\right)+P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})<\underline{c}_{0}\right)

Applying Chebyshev’s inequality and the conditional independence, I know that, for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
,

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\geq\underline{c}_{0}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,YX|W}}[(X-m_{02}(W))^{4}|W=w_{i}]=o(1),

where the second equality applies the Strong Law of Large Numbers to the sample average

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,YX|W}}[(X-m_{02}(W))^{4}|W=w_{i}],

which is valid because of Part 4 of Assumption
1
and the assumption that
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is an i.i.d sequence. Applying Part 2 of the lemma, I deduce that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}(\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0}))<0\right)\longrightarrow 0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. It then follows that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)<\underline{c}_{0}\right)\longrightarrow 0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. A symmetric argument establishes

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)>\overline{c}_{0}\right)\longrightarrow 0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 4.
Since
p_{\beta,m}(Y_{i},X_{i}|w_{i})
is the density of
\mathcal{N}(m(w_{i}),V(\beta))
, I obtain the following expansion of the log-likelihood function around the true parameter
\beta_{0}
:

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m),

and, as a result, I apply the triangle inequality to conclude

\displaystyle\left|\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})+\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|\cdot\left|\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\right|+\frac{n}{2}(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\left|\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|\cdot M_{n}+\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{2}\left|\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\right|

Since
M_{n}\rightarrow\infty
arbitrarily slowly as
n\rightarrow\infty
, it follows from Part 1 and Part 2 of the lemma that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|\cdot M_{n}\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

and

\displaystyle\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{2}\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left|\tilde{I}_{n}(m)-\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This concludes the proof because it implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,m)\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})\times(B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})\cap\mathcal{M}_{n})}\left|R_{n}(\beta,m)\right|\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2
.

The proof is comprised of three steps. Step 1 obtains an expression for the posterior based on a quadratic expansion of the log-likelihood function. Step 2 shows that the numerator is uniformly bounded from above by
\exp(-(\underline{c}_{0}/4)M_{n}^{2})
with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching
1
, where
\underline{c}_{0}>0
is the constant defined in Part 3 of Lemma
1
. Step 3 shows that the denominator is uniformly bounded from below by
\exp(-(\underline{c}_{0}/8)M_{n}^{2})
with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching
1
. The arguments for Step 2 and Step 3 are similar to those encountered on pp. 233-234 and the proof of Lemma 6.3, respectively, in
Bickel and Kleijn 2012
with appropriate modifications.

Step 1.
I start with the definition of the posterior:

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n},m\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\exp\left(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)}{\int_{\mathcal{B}}\exp\left(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)}.

As
p_{\beta,m}(Y_{i},X_{i}|w_{i})
is the density of
\mathcal{N}(m(w_{i}),V(\beta))
, I obtain the following expansion of the log-likelihood function around the true parameter
\beta_{0}

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\Pi\left(\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n},m\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)}{\int_{\mathcal{B}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)}.

Step 2.
Let
A_{n,1}
be the event that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})\cap\mathcal{M}_{n}}\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}M_{n}^{2}}{4}\right)

The first task is to show that
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n,1}^{c})\rightarrow 0
for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Using the definition of the supremum and that probabilities are bounded from above by
1
, I obtain the following string of inequalities

\displaystyle\int_{B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp\left(\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp\left(\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\left(\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\right|\cdot|\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})|-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)\right)

(14)

for all
m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})
. From Part 1 of Lemma
1
, I know that

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|\overset{P_{0,XY|W}^{(n)}}{\longrightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, an application of Chebyshev’s inequality and the Strong Law of Large Numbers implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})=O_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Indeed, for any
\tau>0
, independence across
i
, Chebyshev’s inequality, Part 1 of Assumption
1
, and the Law of Iterated Expectations implies that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|\geq\tau\right)\leq\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E[U^{2}\varepsilon_{2}^{2}|W=w_{i}]=\frac{\sigma_{01}^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Var(\varepsilon_{2}|W=w_{i}),

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})=O_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
after applying the Strong Law of Large Numbers to the sample average
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Var(\varepsilon_{2}|W=w_{i})
, which is valid by Part 2 of Assumption
1
and the fact that
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is i.i.d. Consequently, I am able to conclude that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\sup_{m\in B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})\cap\mathcal{M}_{n}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\right|<\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{4}M_{n}\right)\longrightarrow 1.

(15)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Further, Part 3 of Lemma
1
implies that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\geq\underline{c}_{0}\right)\longrightarrow 1

(16)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Hence, I can combine (
14
), (
15
), and (
16
) to conclude that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n,1}^{c})\leq\mathbf{1}\left\{\underline{c}_{0}\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\left(\frac{1}{4}M_{n}\cdot|\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})|-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)>-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{4}M_{n}^{2}\right\}+o(1).

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Defining
t=\sqrt{n}|\beta-\beta_{0}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\left[\frac{M_{n}}{4}\sqrt{n}|\beta-\beta_{0}|-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right]=\sup_{t\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(0,M_{n})^{c}}\left[\frac{M_{n}}{4}t-\frac{1}{2}t^{2}\right]=-\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{4}

as a concave quadratic function maximized subject to the constraint that
t\geq M_{n}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\left\{\underline{c}_{0}\sup_{\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}}\left(\frac{1}{4}M_{n}\cdot|\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})|-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\right)>-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{4}M_{n}^{2}\right\}=0

and leads to the conclusion that
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n,1}^{c})\longrightarrow 0
for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and, as a result, the complement rule implies
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n,1})\rightarrow 1
as
n\rightarrow\infty
conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 3.
Let
A_{n,2}
be the event that

\displaystyle\inf_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})\cap\mathcal{M}_{n}}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)\geq\exp\left(-\underline{c}_{0}M_{n}^{2}/{8}\right).

I want to show that
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n,2}^{c})\rightarrow 0
for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
because, combined with the result from
Step 2
, it is sufficient to conclude the result of the lemma because it implies

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\Pi\left(\beta\in B_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta_{0},M_{n}/\sqrt{n})^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n},m\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{8}M_{n}^{2}\right)

with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching one for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Let
C=\underline{c}_{0}/8\overline{c}_{0}
with
0<\underline{c}_{0}<\overline{c}_{0}<\infty
defined as in Part 3 of Lemma 1. For this step, I elect to work in the local parametrization
s=\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})
. Consequently, I let
\Pi_{n}
be the induced prior for
s
(i.e., the pushforward of
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}
through the map
\beta\mapsto\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})
), let
\mathcal{S}
be the support of
\Pi_{n}
, and let
\mathcal{S}_{C}=\{s:|s|\leq\sqrt{C}\}
. Under Assumption 2, for large
n
, the induced prior
\Pi_{n}
admits a Lebesgue density
\pi_{n}
such that
\inf_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{C}}\pi_{n}(s)\geq\tilde{C}
for some large constant
\tilde{C}>0
. Consequently, I apply a change of variables and conclude that for
n
large,

\displaystyle\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)

\displaystyle\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\int_{\mathcal{S}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)s-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)d\Pi_{n}(s)

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{C}\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{C})\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)s-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\right)\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds,

where
\lambda
is the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
and
\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)=\mathbf{1}\{s\in\mathcal{S}_{C}\}/\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{C})
is the density of a uniform distribution on
\mathcal{S}_{C}
. Applying Part 3 of Lemma 1, it follows that, for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, the following holds:

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\sup_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M},\infty}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\leq\overline{c}_{0}M_{n}^{2}\right)\rightarrow 1

for any
M_{n}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Using that
C=\underline{c}_{0}/8\overline{c}_{0}
, it then follows that, for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, the following is true:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}s^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m)\geq-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{16}M_{n}^{2}\quad\forall\ (s,m)\in\mathcal{S}_{C}\times(\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n}))

with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching
1
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently,

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(A_{n,2}^{c}\right)\leq P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(\tilde{A}_{n,2}^{c})+o(1),

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
\tilde{A}_{n,2}
is the event

\displaystyle\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)s\right)\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\geq\left(\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{C})\tilde{C}\right)^{-1}\exp\left(-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{16}M_{n}^{2}\right).

Applying Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)s\right)\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\geq\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right)

for all
m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})
.
As the infimum is the greatest lower bound, I then obtain

\displaystyle\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)s\right)\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds

\displaystyle\quad\geq\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\exp\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right),

where the last inequality adds/subtracts
(1/\sqrt{n})\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})
and uses that the sum of the infima is less than or equal to the infimum of the sum. Since

\displaystyle\left|\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right|\leq\int|s|\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\leq\sqrt{C}

because
\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}
is supported on
\{s:|s|\leq\sqrt{C}\}
and
\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}
integrates to
1
(i.e., it is a probability density function), it follows from Part 1 of Lemma
1
that

\displaystyle\exp\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right)\right)=1+o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1)

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. As such, I arrive at the conclusion

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})}\int_{\mathcal{B}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m))d\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}(\beta)<\exp\left(-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{8}M_{n}^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds<-\log\tilde{C}-\log\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{C})-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{16}M_{n}^{2}\right)+o(1).

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since the inequality

\displaystyle-\log\tilde{C}-\log\lambda(\mathcal{S}_{C})\leq\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{32}M_{n}^{2}

holds for
n
large, I can further conclude

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(A_{n,2}^{c}\right)\leq P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds<-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{32}M_{n}^{2}\right)+o(1).

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Next, the event

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds<-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{32}M_{n}^{2}

implies

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right|>\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{32}M_{n}^{2},

and, as a result, I can apply Chebyshev’s inequality to conclude that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds<-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{32}M_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{M_{n}^{4}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|^{2}\left|\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{M_{n}^{4}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|^{2}

with the second inequality using Jensen’s inequality and that
\sup_{s\in\mathcal{S}_{C}}|s|^{2}\leq C.
Since the random variable
\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})
satisfies
E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}[\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})]=0
, it follows that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right|^{2}=Var_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)=\frac{1}{\sigma_{01}^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E_{P_{0,YX|W}}[\varepsilon_{2}^{2}|W=w_{i}]=O(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, where the last equality reflects Part 2 of Assumption 1 and the Strong Law of Large Numbers. Consequently, I obtain

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\int s\pi_{\mathcal{S}_{C}}(s)ds<-\frac{\underline{c}_{0}}{32}M_{n}^{2}\right)=O(M_{n}^{-4}),

as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and, as a result,
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n,2}^{c})\rightarrow 0
for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
because
M_{n}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since I chose the sequence
\{M_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
with
M_{n}\rightarrow\infty
arbitrarily, the argument holds for any such choice and I conclude the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Propositions
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
, and
5
.

B.1
Proof of Proposition
1
and Proposition
2

Proof of Proposition
1
.

The proof has three steps. First, I show that it suffices to study the uniform behavior of the conditional posterior
\Pi(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n},\beta)
, where the uniformity holds over
\mathcal{B}
. Second, I use the first assumption of the proposition to derive a lower bound for the normalizing constant of the conditional posterior that holds uniformly over
\mathcal{B}
. Third, I combine the second assumption of the proposition with a ‘peeling’ argument to establish the proposition.

Step 1.
Applying the law of total probability and using that probability measures are bounded from above by one, I obtain.

\displaystyle\Pi\left(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}\middle|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{\mathcal{B}}\Pi\left(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}\middle|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n},\beta\right)d\Pi(\beta|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\Pi\left(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}\middle|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n},\beta\right).

Consequently, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\Pi\left(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}\middle|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n},\beta\right)=o_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}(1).

(17)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
almost-every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. This is the purpose of the remaining steps.

Step 2.
I start by showing that the normalizing constant of the conditional posterior satisfies

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)\gtrsim\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2})\right)\longrightarrow 1

(18)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
C>0
is a constant. I first note that the log-(quasi)likelihood ratio process satisfies

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)(Z_{i}-m_{0}(w_{i}))

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))

Since
B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})\subseteq\mathcal{M}
, I can use the nonnegativity of the integrand and the expression for
\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0})
to conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\int_{B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\exp\left(-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{2}\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{max}(V^{-1}(\beta))\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\quad=\exp\left(-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{2}\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{max}(V^{-1}(\beta))\right)\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\int_{B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m),

where
\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}
is the renormalized restriction of
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
to the event
B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})
. Note that the compactness of
\mathcal{B}
, the continuity of
\beta\mapsto V(\beta)
, and the positive-definiteness of
V(\beta)
guarantees
\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{max}(V^{-1}(\beta))<\infty
. I now want to argue that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)\geq\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)\longrightarrow 1

(19)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Indeed, if this holds, then

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{M}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)\geq\exp\left(-\bar{C}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n}))\right)\longrightarrow 1

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
for the choice of constant
\bar{C}=\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{max}(V^{-1}(\beta))+1>0
. This implies (
18
) following an application of Condition 1 in the statement of the proposition. To show (
19
), Jensen’s inequality reveals

\displaystyle\log\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)

Consequently,

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\log\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)<-n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)<-n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\left|\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)\right|>n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}\delta_{n}^{4}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left|\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)\right|^{2},

where the last inequality is Chebyshev’s inequality. Applying Jensen’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem, I know that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left|\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)E_{P_{0,YX|W}}(\varepsilon\varepsilon^{\prime}|W=w_{i})V^{-1}(\beta)(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim n\delta_{n}^{2},

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every sequence
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, where the last inequality uses the maximum eigenvalue assumption
\sup_{w\in\mathcal{W}}\lambda_{max}(E(\varepsilon\varepsilon^{\prime}|W=w))<\infty
as well as the fact that
\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{max}(V^{-1}(\beta))<\infty
by the compactness of
\mathcal{B}
, the continuity of
\beta\mapsto V^{-1}(\beta)
, and the positive definiteness of
V(\beta)
. Since
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
, this implies that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{B}_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n})}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M},\delta_{n}}(m)<\exp(-n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)=o(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This verifies (
19
).

Step 3.
I study the numerator

\displaystyle\int_{B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m).

Since

\displaystyle B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}=\bigcup_{j=0}^{\infty}A_{n,j},\quad A_{n,j}=\{D2^{j}\delta_{n}\leq\max\{||m_{1}-m_{01}||_{n,2},||m_{2}-m_{02}||_{n,2}\}<D2^{j+1}\delta_{n}\},

I can write

\displaystyle\int_{B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{A_{n,j}}\exp(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0}))d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

Using the expression for
\ell_{n}(\beta,m)
, I know that

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0})\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}-D^{2}2^{2j}n\delta_{n}^{2}\inf_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{min}(V^{-1}(\beta))

for all
(\beta,m)\in\mathcal{B}\times A_{n,j}
and all
j\geq 0
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{A_{n,j}}\exp\left(\ell_{n}(\beta,m)-\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0})\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{A_{n,j}}\exp\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}-\tilde{D}2^{2j}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\int_{A_{n,j}}\exp\left(\sqrt{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}-\tilde{D}2^{2j}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m),

where
\tilde{D}=\inf_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\lambda_{min}(V^{-1}(\beta))D^{2}>0
. Therefore, to complete the proof, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sup_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D2^{j+1}\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|\leq\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\ \forall\ j\right)\rightarrow 1

(20)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Indeed, it implies I can condition on the event given in the probability in (
20
) and conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\int_{A_{n,j}}\exp\left(\sqrt{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}-\tilde{D}2^{2j}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\exp\left(\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}n\delta_{n}^{2}-\tilde{D}2^{2j}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{\tilde{D}}{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}2^{2j}\right),

which, when combined with (
18
), implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\Pi(B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\exp\left(\left(C_{1}-\frac{\tilde{D}}{2}2^{2j}\right)n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching one, and, as a result, the claim of the proposition holds if
D>0
is sufficiently large because
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
. To verify (
20
), I apply the union bound, Markov’s inequality, and the assumptions on
\delta\mapsto\omega_{n}(\delta)
to obtain that, for
D>0
large, the following holds:

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\ \exists\ j\ s.t.\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sup_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D2^{j+1}\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|>\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sup_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D2^{j+1}\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|>\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sup_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D2^{j+1}\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{\omega_{n}(D2^{j+1}\delta_{n})}{\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim D^{\upsilon-2}\frac{\omega_{n}(\delta_{n})}{\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{(\upsilon-2)j}.

Since it is assumed that
\omega_{n}(\delta_{n})\leq\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}
, I am then able to conclude that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\ \exists\ j\ s.t.\sup_{\beta\in\mathcal{B}}\sup_{m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D2^{j+1}\delta_{n})}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|>\tilde{D}2^{2j-1}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\right)=o(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
provided that
D\rightarrow\infty
. Hence, by setting
D>0
large (i.e.,
D=D_{n}
with
D_{n}\rightarrow\infty
arbitrarily slowly as
n\rightarrow\infty
), I verify (
20
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Since
\Pi(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, the law of total probability reveals that

\displaystyle\Pi\left(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}\middle|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)=\Pi\left(m\in\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}\middle|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)+o_{P_{0,YX}^{(n)}}(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
almost-every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Consequently, the claim of the proposition holds by repeating the steps of Proposition
1
with the only modification being that Step 3 uses
\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}
instead of
B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},D\delta_{n})^{c}
.
∎

B.2
Proof of Proposition
3

Proof of Proposition
3
.

It suffices to verify Assumptions
3
and
4
. Step 1 and Step 2 verify the conditions underpinning Proposition
1
so that Assumption
3
is verified. Step 3 verifies Assumption
4
.

Step 1.
I first verify Part 1 of Proposition
1
for the sequence
\delta_{n}=\max\{\delta_{n,1},\delta_{n,2}\}
, where

\displaystyle\delta_{n,j}=\left(\frac{\log n}{n}\right)^{\frac{\alpha_{0,j}}{2\alpha_{0,j}+1}}

for each
j\in\{1,2\}
. To that end, it suffices to show that, for each
j\in\{1,2\}
, there is a constant
C_{1,j}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(||m_{j}-m_{0,j}||_{\infty}<\delta_{n,j})\geq\exp(-C_{1,j}n\delta_{n,j}^{2}).

(21)

Indeed,
||\cdot||_{n,2}\leq||\cdot||_{\infty}
and
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}=\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}\otimes\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}
implies that if (
21
) holds, then, for
C_{1}=C_{1,1}+C_{1,2}
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n}))\geq\exp\left(-C_{1}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, which is Part 1 of Proposition
1
. To verify (
21
), I follow the argument of Proposition 1 in
Giné and Nickl 2011
with appropriate modifications. Let
m_{0,lk,j}
be the coefficients in the wavelet series representation of
m_{0,j}
. Since the supremum norm of the difference
m_{0,j}-m_{j}
satisfies
||m_{0,j}-m_{j}||_{\infty}\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{l/2}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}|m_{0,lk,j}-2^{-l(\alpha_{0,j}+1/2)}\nu_{lk,j}|
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(||m_{j}-m_{0,j}||_{\infty}<\delta_{n,j})\geq\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{l/2}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}|2^{-l(\alpha_{0,j}+1/2)}\nu_{lk,j}-m_{0,lk,j}|<\delta_{n,j}\right)

Next, I write
\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}=2^{l(\alpha_{0j}+1/2)}m_{0,lk,j}
and note that
|\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|\leq||m_{0,j}||_{\infty,\infty,\alpha_{0,j}}\leq M
to obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{l/2}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}|2^{-l(\alpha_{0,j}+1/2)}\nu_{lk,j}-m_{0,lk,j}|

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{l=0}^{L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|+\sum_{l=L+1}^{\infty}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{l=0}^{L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|+2M\sum_{l=L+1}^{\infty}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{l=0}^{L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|+2^{-L\alpha_{0,j}}c_{1,j}

where
c_{1,j}>0
is a constant that depends on
M
and
\alpha_{0,j}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|<\delta_{n,j}-2^{-L\alpha_{0,j}}c_{1,j}\implies||m_{j}-m_{0,j}||_{\infty}<\delta_{n,j}.

Now, if
L
is chosen such that
2^{L}
is of the order
(n/\log n)^{1/(2\alpha_{0,j}+1)}
, then I can use the above to conclude that, for
n
large, there exists constants
c_{2,j},c_{3,j}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\left(||m_{j}-m_{0,j}||_{\infty}<\delta_{n,j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}2^{-l\alpha_{0,j}}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|<c_{2,j}\delta_{n,j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\left(\max_{0\leq l\leq L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|<c_{3,j}\delta_{n,j}\right),

Let
S(L)=\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{l}-1}1\leq 2\cdot 2^{L}
. Since
\nu_{lk,j}
are i.i.d
U(-M,M)
random variables and
2^{L}
is of the order
\delta_{n,j}^{-(1/\alpha_{0,j})}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\left(\max_{0\leq l\leq L}\max_{0\leq k\leq 2^{l}-1}|\nu_{lk,j}-\tilde{m}_{0,lk,j}|<c_{3,j}\delta_{n,j}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{c_{3,j}\delta_{n,j}}{2M}\right)^{S(L)}

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-c_{4,j}\log(1/\delta_{n,j})\delta_{n,j}^{-\frac{1}{\alpha_{0,j}}})

\displaystyle\geq\exp(-C_{1,j}n\delta_{n,j}^{2})

where
c_{4,j},C_{1,j}>0
are constants. This verifies Part 1 of Assumption
1
. Since
\alpha_{0,1},\alpha_{0,2}>1/2
,
\delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/4})
.

Step 2.
I verify Part 2 of Proposition
1
. Since
\mathcal{B}
is compact and
\beta\mapsto V^{-1}(\beta)
is continuous, an application of the triangle inequality reveals that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{(\beta,m)\in\mathcal{B}\times B_{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta)}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sum_{(j_{1},j_{2})\in\{1,2\}^{2}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{m_{j_{1}}\in\mathcal{M}_{j_{1}}:||m_{j_{1}}-m_{0,j_{1}}||_{n,2}\leq\delta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{j_{1}}(w_{i})-m_{0,j_{1}}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i,j_{2}}\right|\right]

Since
\varepsilon_{i,j_{2}}
is subgaussian conditional on
W
for all
j_{2}\in\{1,2\}
, Corollary 2.2.9 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 2023
yields the following bound

\displaystyle\sum_{(j_{1},j_{2})\in\{1,2\}^{2}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{m_{j_{1}}\in\mathcal{M}_{j_{1}}:||m_{j_{1}}-m_{0,j_{1}}||_{n,2}\leq\delta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{j_{1}}(w_{i})-m_{0,j_{1}}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i,j_{2}}\right|\right]\lesssim\omega_{n}(\delta),

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, where

\displaystyle\omega_{n}(\delta)=\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{1},||\cdot||_{n,2})}d\tau+\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{2},||\cdot||_{n,2})}d\tau.

Since
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
are balls in the Besov spaces
B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha_{0,1}}([0,1])
and
B_{\infty,\infty}^{\alpha_{0,2}}([0,1])
, respectively, and
||\cdot||_{n,2}\leq||\cdot||_{\infty}
, I apply Theorem 4.3.36 of
Giné and Nickl 2015
to conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{j},||\cdot||_{n,2})}d\tau\leq\delta^{1-\frac{1}{2\alpha_{0,j}}}.

for each
j\in\{1,2\}
. Consequently, any sequence
\{\bar{\delta}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
for which
\bar{\delta}_{n}\geq n^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{(2\alpha_{0}+1)}}
with
\alpha_{0}=\min\{\alpha_{0,1},\alpha_{0,2}\}
satisfies Part 2 of Proposition
1
. Consequently,
\delta_{n}=(n/\log n)^{-\alpha_{0}/(2\alpha_{0}+1)}
satisfies Part 2 of Proposition
1
.

Step 3.
The argument of Step 2 establishes

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{m_{j_{1}}\in\mathcal{M}_{j_{1}}:||m_{j_{1}}-m_{0,j_{1}}||_{n,2}\leq\delta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{j_{1}}(w_{i})-m_{0,j_{1}}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i,j_{2}}\right|\right]\leq\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\ \forall\ j_{1},j_{2}\in\{1,2\}

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Consequently, Assumption
4
is verified because
U=\varepsilon_{1}-\beta_{0}\varepsilon_{2}
is a linear combination of
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
and
\delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/4})
implies
\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

B.3
Proof of Propositions
4
and
5

I start with a lemma that is used to verify the second condition in Proposition
2
. It is the only point where the normality assumption in Proposition
4
is used (in particular, it is used in
Step 2
of the proof).

Lemma 3
.

Suppose that the following conditions hold

1.

The distribution of the data satisfies
(Y,X)|W\sim\mathcal{N}(m_{0}(W),V(\beta_{0}))

2.

There exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that the prior
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
satisfies
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m\in B_{n,\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta_{n}))\geq\exp(-C_{1}n\delta_{n}^{2})
for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
.

3.

There exists sets
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c})\leq\exp(-Mn\delta_{n}^{2})
for some
M>0
large.

4.

The sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
satisfies
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
and
\delta_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Then, for prior
\Pi=\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
with
\Pi_{\mathcal{B}}
being the uniform distribution on
[-B,B]
and
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
being the prior described above, the posterior satisfies

\displaystyle\Pi(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\overset{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}{\rightarrow}0

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof.

The proof has two steps. The first step uses Condition 2 to show that the normalizing constant of the posterior is bounded from below by
\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2})
with
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability approaching one as
n\rightarrow\infty
. The second step uses the first step and Conditions 1,3, and 4 to conclude the result. Note that Step 2 is the only part of the argument in which correct specification (i.e., Condition 1) is used.

Step 1.
I start with the normalizing constant in the posterior. Since

\displaystyle\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}=\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}\cdot\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})},

I know that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2B}\int_{[-B,B]}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)d\beta

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\beta\in[-B,B]}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{1}{2B}\int_{[-B,B]}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta

Step 2 of the proof of Proposition
1
yields that there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\inf_{\beta\in[-B,B]}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)\geq\exp(-C_{1}n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)\longrightarrow 1.

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed sequence
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Now, I show that there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\frac{1}{2B}\int_{[-B,B]}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta\geq\exp(-C_{2}n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)\longrightarrow 1

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed sequence
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. By the nonnegativity of the integrand and the assumption that
\delta_{n}=o(1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{[-B,B]}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n}\}}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta

for large
n
. Using the expression for
\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0})
, I know that

\displaystyle\ell_{n}(\beta,m_{0})-\ell_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})-\frac{1}{2}n(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})-\frac{n\delta_{n}^{2}}{2}\tilde{I}_{n}(m_{0})

By Part 3 of Lemma
1
, I know that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{[-B,B]}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta\geq\exp\left(-\frac{\overline{c}_{0}}{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\int_{\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n}\}}\exp\left(\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)d\beta\right)\rightarrow 1

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Now, letting
\lambda(\cdot)
denote the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}
, I know that

\displaystyle\int_{\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n}\}}\exp\left(\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)d\beta

\displaystyle\quad=\lambda(\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n})\int\exp\left(\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)\pi_{\delta_{n}}(\beta)d\beta,

where
\pi_{\delta_{n}}(\beta)
is the uniform distribution on
\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n}\}
. An application of Chebyshev’s inequality and Jensen’s inequality reveals that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int\exp\left(\sqrt{n}(\beta-\beta_{0})\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\tilde{\ell}_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})\right)\pi_{\delta_{n}}(\beta)d\beta<\exp\left(-\frac{\overline{c}_{0}}{2}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\right)=o(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Consequently, the following holds

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{[-B,B]}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta\geq\exp\left(-\overline{c}_{0}n\delta_{n}^{2}\right)\lambda(\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n}\})\right)=1+o(1)

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Since
\lambda(\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}:|\beta-\beta_{0}|<\delta_{n}\})=2\delta_{n}
and
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
, it follows that

\displaystyle P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}\left(\int_{[-B,B]}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m_{0})}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\beta\geq\exp\left(-(\overline{c}_{0}+1)n\delta_{n}^{2}\right))\right)\longrightarrow 1

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Define
C_{2}=\overline{c}_{0}+1
to obtain the desired inequality.

Step 2.
By Step 1, I can condition on the event

\displaystyle A_{n}=\left\{\frac{1}{2B}\int_{[-B,B]}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)d\beta\geq\exp(-Cn\delta_{n}^{2})\right\},

where
C=C_{1}+C_{2}
.
Consequently, I deduce that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\mathbf{1}\{A_{n}\}\right]+o(1)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\exp(Cn\delta_{n}^{2})\int_{[-B,B]}\int_{\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\frac{L_{n}(\beta,m)}{L_{n}(\beta_{0},m_{0})}d\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(m)d\beta+o(1)

\displaystyle\quad=\exp(Cn\delta_{n}^{2})\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c})+o(1),

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every sequence
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, where the first inequality uses the law of total probability and the conclusion of Step 1 (i.e.,
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}(A_{n}^{c})=o(1)
), the second inequality uses
A_{n}
and Fubini’s theorem, and the equality uses the assumption of correct specification (i.e.,
P_{0,YX|W}=\mathcal{N}(m_{0}(W),V(\beta_{0}))
). Since
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}
satisfies
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c})\lesssim\exp(-Mn\delta_{n}^{2})
for
M>0
, I can find a constant
\tilde{C}>0
such that the following holds

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\right]\lesssim\exp(-\tilde{C}n\delta_{n}^{2})+o(1).

for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every sequence
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Since
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
and convergence in mean implies convergence in probability, the proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4
.

The proof has four steps. Step 1 proposes a candidate sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
that satisfies Parts 1 and 4 of Proposition
2
. Steps 2 and 3 then verify Parts 2 and 3, respectively, to verify Assumption
3
. Step 4 verifies Assumption
4
.

Step 1.
Applying Lemma 5.3 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten 2008b
, the marginal prior
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}
satisfies the inequality
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(||m_{j}-m_{0j}||_{\infty}<\delta)\geq\exp(-\varphi_{m_{0,j},j}(\delta/2))
for each
j\in\{1,2\}
and any
\delta>0
, where
\delta\mapsto\varphi_{m_{0,j},j}(\delta)
for
\delta>0
is the supremum norm concentration function of the Matérn Gaussian process at
m_{0,j}
. That is,
\varphi_{m_{0,j},j}(\delta)=\inf_{h_{j}\in\mathbb{H}_{j}:||h_{j}-m_{0,j}||_{\infty}<\delta}\frac{1}{2}||h_{j}||_{\mathbb{H}_{j}}-\log\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(||m_{j}||_{\infty}<\delta)
, for each
\delta>0
, where
(\mathbb{H}_{j},||\cdot||_{\mathbb{H}_{j}})
is the reproducing kernel Hilbert space (RKHS) associated with the Matern Gaussian process
m_{j}
. Applying Lemma 3 and 4 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten 2011
, the minimal sequence
\tilde{\delta}_{n,j}
for which
\varphi_{m_{0,j},j}(\tilde{\delta}_{n,j})\leq n\tilde{\delta}_{n,j}^{2}
is
\tilde{\delta}_{n,j}=n^{-\min\{\alpha_{j},\alpha_{0,j}\}/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}
, however, since I only require
\delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/4})
, it is without loss of generality to set
\delta_{n,j}=\max\{\tilde{\delta}_{n,j},n^{-\alpha_{j}/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}\}
because
\alpha_{j}>d_{w}/2
guarantees that
n^{-\alpha_{j}/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}=o(n^{-1/4})
(in addition to
\alpha_{0,j}>\alpha_{j}/2+d_{w}/4
guaranteeing
\tilde{\delta}_{n,j}=o(n^{-1/4})
too). Hence, a candidate is
\delta_{n}=\max\{\delta_{n,1},\delta_{n,2}\}
and it meets the requirement that
\delta_{n}=o(n^{-1/4})
and Part 1 of Proposition
2
because
||\cdot||_{n,2}\leq||\cdot||_{\infty}
and
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}=\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}\otimes\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}
. Notice that
n\delta_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, so Part 4 of Proposition
2
holds too. It remains to verify Parts 2 and 3 of Proposition
2
in order to check Assumption
3
. This is the task of the next two steps.

Step 2.
I construct sets
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
such that Part 2 of Proposition
2
holds. These sets are similar to those encountered in Theorem 3 of
de Jonge and van Zanten 2013
. For each
n\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{M}_{n}=\mathcal{M}_{n1}\times\mathcal{M}_{n2}
with
\mathcal{M}_{nj}=M_{j}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\mathbb{H}_{j,1}+\gamma_{n,j}C_{1}^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}})
for each
j\in\{1,2\}
, where
\mathbb{H}_{j,1}=\{f\in\mathbb{H}_{j}:||f||_{\mathbb{H}_{j}}\leq 1\}
is the unit ball in
(\mathbb{H}_{j},||\cdot||_{\mathbb{H}_{j}})
,
C_{1}^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}})=\{f\in C^{a_{j}}([0,1]):||f||_{a_{j}}\leq 1\}
is the unit ball in
(C^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}}),||\cdot||_{a_{j}})
for some
a_{j}\in(d_{w}/2,\alpha_{j})
,
\gamma_{n,j}=n^{-(\alpha_{j}-a_{j})/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}
, and
M_{j}>0
is a sufficiently large constant for each
j\in\{1,2\}
. Since
m_{j}
takes values in
(C^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}}),||\cdot||_{a_{j}})
for any
a_{j}<\alpha_{j}
, I can view
m_{j}
as a Gaussian element in the Banach space
(C^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}}),||\cdot||_{a_{j}})
for
a_{j}\in(d_{w}/2,\alpha_{j})
. Such an
a_{j}
exists because
\alpha_{j}>d_{w}/2
by the assumption of the proposition. The RKHS is unchanged because
C([0,1]^{d_{w}})
is the completion of
C^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}})
with respect to the uniform norm
||\cdot||_{\infty}
and
||\cdot||_{\infty}\leq||\cdot||_{a_{j}}
(see Lemma 8.1 in
van der Vaart and van Zanten 2008b
). I then apply Theorem 5.1 in
van der Vaart and van Zanten 2008b
to conclude that the law
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}
satisfies the inequality
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\mathcal{M}_{nj}^{c})\leq 1-\Phi\left(\Phi^{-1}\left(\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(m_{j}\in\gamma_{n,j}C_{1}^{a_{j}}([0,1]^{d_{w}}))\right)+M_{j}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right)
, where
\Phi(\cdot)
denotes the standard normal cumulative distribution function. Since the unit ball
\mathbb{H}_{1,j}
in
(\mathbb{H}_{j},||\cdot||_{\mathbb{H}_{j}})
satisfies the metric entropy condition
\log N(\tau,\mathbb{H}_{1,j},||\cdot||_{a_{j}})\lesssim\tau^{-2d_{w}/(d_{w}+2(\alpha_{j}-a_{j}))}
for any
\tau>0
(see Section 4.3 of
van der Vaart and van Zanten 2011
), I can invoke the relationship between the metric entropy of the unit ball in RKHS and small ball probabilities established in
Li and Linde 1999
to find a constant
L_{j}>0
such that
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(||m_{j}||_{a_{j}}<\gamma_{n,j})\geq\exp(-L_{j}\gamma_{n,j}^{-d_{w}/(\alpha_{j}-a_{j})})
for each
j\in\{1,2\}
. Since
\gamma_{n,j}=n^{-(\alpha_{j}-a_{j})/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}
satisfies
\gamma_{n,j}^{-d_{w}/(\alpha_{j}-a_{j})}=n^{d_{w}/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}\leq n\delta_{n}^{2}
because of the lower bound
\delta_{n}\geq n^{-\alpha_{j}/(2\alpha_{j}+d_{w})}
, it follows that
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(||m_{j}||_{a_{j}}<\gamma_{n,j})\geq\exp(-L_{j}n\delta_{n}^{2})
. Consequently,
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}
satisfies
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\mathcal{M}_{nj}^{c})\leq 1-\Phi\left(\Phi^{-1}\left(\exp(-L_{j}n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)+M_{j}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\right)
for each
j\in\{1,2\}
. Using the inequality
\Phi^{-1}(p)\geq-\sqrt{2\log(1/p)}
for any
p\in(0,1)
, I know
\Phi^{-1}\left(\exp(-L_{j}n\delta_{n}^{2})\right)\geq-\sqrt{2L_{j}n\delta_{n}^{2}}=-\tilde{L}_{j}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}

for
\tilde{L}_{j}=\sqrt{2L_{j}}
. Consequently,
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\mathcal{M}_{nj}^{c})\lesssim 1-\Phi((M_{j}-\tilde{L}_{j})\sqrt{n}\delta_{n})
. By choosing
M_{j}>\tilde{L}_{j}
arbitrarily large and using the inequality
\Phi(z)\geq 1-\exp(-z^{2}/2)
, it follows that
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}(\mathcal{M}_{nj}^{c})\lesssim\exp(-\frac{1}{2}(M_{j}-\tilde{L}_{j})^{2}n\delta_{n}^{2})
for each
j\in\{1,2\}
. Since
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c})\lesssim\max\{\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}(\mathcal{M}_{n1}^{c}),\Pi_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}(\mathcal{M}_{n2}^{c})\}
, it follows that there exists a large constant
M>0
such that
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c})\lesssim\exp(-Mn\delta_{n}^{2})
. Applying Lemma
3
, I then conclude that the posterior satisfies
\Pi_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{M}_{n}^{c}|\{(Y_{i},X_{i},w_{i}^{\prime})^{\prime}\}_{i=1}^{n})\rightarrow 0
in
P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}
-probability for
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every fixed realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This verifies Part 2 of Proposition
2
.

Step 3.
I verify Part 3 of Proposition
2
. Since
\mathcal{B}
is compact and
\beta\mapsto V^{-1}(\beta)
is continuous, an application of the triangle inequality reveals that

\displaystyle E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{(\beta,m)\in\mathcal{B}\times(\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B_{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0},\delta))}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m(w_{i})-m_{0}(w_{i}))^{\prime}V^{-1}(\beta)\varepsilon_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\sum_{(j_{1},j_{2})\in\{1,2\}^{2}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{m_{j_{1}}\in\mathcal{M}_{n,j_{1}}:||m_{j_{1}}-m_{0,j_{1}}||_{n,2}\leq\delta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{j_{1}}(w_{i})-m_{0,j_{1}}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i,j_{2}}\right|\right]

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of the sequence
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
. Since
\varepsilon_{j_{2}}
is subgaussian conditional on
W
for all
j_{2}\in\{1,2\}
, Corollary 2.2.9 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 2023
implies

\displaystyle\sum_{(j_{1},j_{2})\in\{1,2\}^{2}}E_{P_{0,YX|W}^{(n)}}\left[\sup_{m_{j_{1}}\in\mathcal{M}_{n,j_{1}}:||m_{j_{1}}-m_{0,j_{1}}||_{n,2}\leq\delta}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(m_{j_{1}}(w_{i})-m_{0,j_{1}}(w_{i}))\varepsilon_{i,j_{2}}\right|\right]\lesssim\omega_{n}(\delta),

conditionally given
P_{0,W}^{\infty}
-almost every realization
\{w_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
of the sequence
\{W_{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
, where

\displaystyle\omega_{n}(\delta)=\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{n,1},||\cdot||_{n,2})}d\tau+\int_{0}^{\delta}\sqrt{\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{n,2},||\cdot||_{n,2})}d\tau

I show that
\omega_{n}(\delta_{n})\lesssim\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}
for the choice of
\delta_{n}
proposed in Step 1. Using the definition of
\mathcal{M}_{nj}
and
||\cdot||_{n,2}\leq||\cdot||_{\infty}
, I deduce that

\displaystyle\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{n,j},||\cdot||_{n,2})\leq\log N(\tau/2,M_{j}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\mathbb{H}_{j,1},||\cdot||_{\infty})+\log N(\tau/2,\gamma_{n,j}C_{1}^{a_{j}}([0,1]),||\cdot||_{\infty}),

and, as a result, I can use the metric entropy bound of the RKHS unit ball
\mathbb{H}_{j,1}
stated in Step 2 and Theorem 2.7.1 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 2023
to conclude that

\displaystyle\log N(\tau,M_{j}\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}\mathbb{H}_{j,1},||\cdot||_{\infty})\lesssim\left(\frac{\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{2d_{w}}{2\alpha_{j}+d_{w}}}

and

\displaystyle\log N(\tau,\gamma_{n,j}C_{1}^{a_{j}}([0,1]),||\cdot||_{\infty})\lesssim\left(\frac{\gamma_{n,j}}{\tau}\right)^{\frac{d_{w}}{a_{j}}}.

This implies that the metric entropy integral satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\delta_{n}}\sqrt{\log N(\tau,\mathcal{M}_{n,j},||\cdot||_{\infty})}d\tau\lesssim n^{\frac{d_{w}}{4\alpha_{j}+2d_{w}}}\delta_{n}+\gamma_{n,j}^{\frac{d_{w}}{2a_{j}}}\delta_{n}^{\frac{2a_{j}-d_{w}}{2a_{j}}}.

Calculations reveal that
n^{\frac{d_{w}}{4\alpha_{j}+2d_{w}}}\delta_{n}+\gamma_{n,j}^{\frac{d_{w}}{2a_{j}}}\delta_{n}^{\frac{2a_{j}-d_{w}}{2a_{j}}}\lesssim\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}\ \forall\ j\in\{1,2\}
, so
\delta_{n}
satisfies Part 3 of Proposition
2
.

Step 4.
I now verify Assumption
4
for
\mathcal{M}_{n}\cap B(m_{0},D\delta_{n})
, where
\{\mathcal{M}_{n}\}_{n\geq 1}
is the sequence introduced in Step 2. Since
U=\varepsilon_{1}-\beta_{0}\varepsilon_{2}
and
\sqrt{n}\delta_{n}^{2}=o(1)
, the maximal inequalities from Step 3 also verify Assumption
4
through an application of the triangle inequality. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5
.

It suffices to verify the conditions of Theorem 12.9 of
Ghosal and Van der Vaart 2017
. This involves four steps. Step 1 proposes a subsets of the parameter space
[-B,B]\times\mathcal{H}
for which the posterior concentrates as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Step 2 verifies a LAN condition along the sets from Step 1 to check condition (12.13) of
Ghosal and Van der Vaart 2017
. Step 3 uses the subsets from Step 1 to verify condition (12.14) of
Ghosal and Van der Vaart 2017
. Step 4 establishes the claim of the proposition by applying Theorem 12.9 of
Ghosal and Van der Vaart 2017
. Since Theorem 12.9 in
Ghosal and Van der Vaart 2017
is an extension of Theorem 2 in
Castillo 2012a
, these steps also correspond to verifying the conditions of his theorem and I will make this connection throughout the argument.

Step 1.
In order to verify Theorem 12.9 of
Ghosal and Van der Vaart 2017
